巴特利特船长、古德塞尔医生、轮机长沃德维尔、珀西——他们尽可以谈他们的；而我心里始终盘踞不去的一个念头，就是马文，就是他的死。我想起他，想起他对我的种种好处；他那寡居的母亲苦苦等候儿子归来的景象，无时无刻不浮现在我眼前。我想起自己的母亲，我对她几乎没有什么记忆；我由衷地但愿，被带走的是我才好。麦克米伦和博鲁普回来之后，我从库德卢克图和哈里根那里听悉了这件惨事的始末。我总觉得，倘若当时陪在他身边的是文明的同伴，马文之死这个悲伤的故事本不必有人来讲。
拔营的时候，他先上了路，把装橇的活儿留给小伙子们，让他们装好之后跟着来。他们要南下回陆地、回船，他无须同他们守在一处；待他们赶到他消失的地方，只见他四周已结起新冰，头和脚都没在冰下，露在外面的，唯有背上和肩头的毛皮衣裳。他们没有设法抢救他；即便把他的遗体捞了上来，救活的希望也微乎其微，因为气温远在零下，而对如何救活溺水之人，他们一无所知。对他那几个孩子气的小伙伴，谁也无可责难。
他孤独地死去，又孤独地、勇敢而光荣地走进了那伟大的未知。他是尘世伟大殉道者中的最后一位；他到家了；他的工作已经完成；他正在他一心渴望的地方；水手已在海上归家。他的坟墓是地球上最北的一座坟，这对身后亲人的慰藉何其微薄；但他们明白，这一牺牲并非虚掷，因为正是亏了他，走在后面的人才得以循着踪迹，重新回到陆地。那几个傻孩子依照因纽特人的传统，把马文教授的随身遗物全都卸在冰上，免得他的魂灵跟着他们走，然后急急忙忙赶回陆地、赶回船上，讲出了他们那个悲伤的故事。

## XVIII / 十八

===Original===
After musk-oxen﻿—The doctor’s scientific expedition.

From the time of my arrival at the *Roosevelt*, for nearly three weeks, my days were spent in complete idleness. I would catch a fleeting glimpse of Commander Peary, but not once in all of that time did he speak a word to me. Then he spoke to me in the most ordinary matter-of-fact way, and ordered me to get to work. Not a word about the North Pole or anything connected with it; simply, “There is enough wood left, and I would like to have you make a couple of sledges and mend the broken ones. I hope you are feeling all right.” There was enough wood left and I made three sledges, as well as repaired those that were broken.

The Commander was still running things and he remained the commander to the last minute; nothing escaped him, and when the time came to slowdown on provisions, he gave the orders, and we had but two spare meals a day to sustain us. The whole expedition lived on travel rations from before the time we left Cape Sheridan until we had reached Sydney, NS, and like the keen-fanged hounds, we were always ready and fit.

===Chinese===
追猎麝牛——医生的科考远征。

自从抵达*罗斯福号*，将近三个星期里，我整天无所事事。皮尔里指挥官的身影偶尔匆匆一瞥，可整整那段时间，他一句话也没有对我说过。后来他开了口，用的却是最平常不过、就事论事的口气，吩咐我去干活。关于北极点以及一切与之相关的事，一个字也没提；只说：「剩下的木料还够，我想让你打几架雪橇，再把破损的修一修。希望你身子骨还好。」木料确实还够，我打制了三架雪橇，还把那些破损的都修好了。

指挥官依旧统管着一切，直到最后一刻他都是我们的指挥官；什么事都逃不过他的眼睛。到了口粮需要紧缩的时候，命令由他下达，我们一天便只剩两顿饭的口粮勉强撑持。全队从离开谢里登角之前起，直到抵达悉尼（新斯科舍省），一路上吃的都是行军口粮；而我们恰似一群尖牙利齿的猎犬，始终整装待命、劲头十足。

===Original===
It was late in May when Prof. MacMillan and Mr. Borup, with their Eskimo companions returned from Cape Jesup, where they had been doing highly important scientific work, taking soundings out on the sea-ice north of the cape as high as 84° 15′ north, and also at the cape. They had made a trip that was record-breaking; they had visited the different cairns made by Lockwood and Brainard and by Commander Peary, and they had also captured and brought into the ship a musk-ox calf; and they had most satisfactorily demonstrated their fitness as Arctic explorers, having followed the Commander’s orders implicitly and secured more than the required number of tidal-readings and soundings.

Prof. MacMillan, with Jack Barnes, a sailor, and Kudlooktoo, left for Fort Conger early in June, and continued the work of tidal-observations. They rejoined the *Roosevelt* just before she left Cape Sheridan. A little later in the month, Borup went to Clements Markham Inlet to hunt musk-oxen, and from there he went to Cape Columbia, where he erected the cairn containing the record of the last and successful expedition of the “Peary Arctic Club.” The cairn was a substantial pile of rocks, surmounted by a strong oaken guidepost, with arms pointing “North 413 miles to the Pole”; “East, to Cape Morris K. Jesup, 275 miles”; “West to Cape Thomas H. Hubbard, 225 miles”; while the southern arm pointed south, but to no particular geographical spot; it was labeled “Cape Columbia.” Underneath the arms of the guidepost, which had been made by Mate Gushue, was a small, glass-covered, boxlike arrangement, in which was encased the record of Peary’s successful journey to the Pole, and the roster of the expedition, my name included. From the crossbars, guys of galvanized wire were stretched and secured to heavy rocks, to help sustain the monument from the fury of the storms. Borup did good work, photographed the result, and the picture of the cairn, when exhibited, proved very satisfactory to the Commander.

Dr. Goodsell with two teams, and the Eskimo men, Keshungwah and Tawchingwah, left the ship on May 27, to hunt in the Lake Hazen and Ruggles River regions. They were successful in securing thirteen musk-oxen in that neighborhood, and in Bellows Valley they shot a number of the “Peary” caribou, the species “*Rangifer Pearyi*,” a distinct class of reindeer inhabiting that region.

===Chinese===
五月末，麦克米伦教授和博鲁普先生带着各自的因纽特同伴从杰瑟普角（Cape Jesup）归来；他们一直在那里从事极为重要的科学工作：在该角以北的海冰上测深，远达北纬84度15分处，在该角本地也做了观测。他们这一趟行程破了纪录；他们寻访了洛克伍德与布雷纳德以及皮尔里指挥官先后垒起的各座石堆；还活捉了一头小麝牛带回船上；他们绝对听从指挥官的命令，取得的潮位读数与测深数据超出了规定数量，极其圆满地证明了自己堪当北极探险家。

六月初，麦克米伦教授带着水手杰克·巴恩斯（Jack Barnes）和库德卢克图动身前往康格堡（Fort Conger），继续潮汐观测工作。就在*罗斯福号*驶离谢里登角之前，他们赶回船来会合。当月再晚些时候，博鲁普前往克莱门茨·马克姆湾猎麝牛，随后又从那里转赴哥伦比亚角，在那里垒起一座石堆，封存着「皮尔里北极俱乐部」这最后一次圆满成功的探险的记录。那石堆是一大座结实的石垒，顶上竖着一根坚固的橡木指路柱，横臂上标示：「北，距北极点413英里」；「东，至莫里斯·K·杰瑟普角（Cape Morris K. Jesup），275英里」；「西，至托马斯·H·哈伯德角（Cape Thomas H. Hubbard），225英里」；朝南的横臂指向正南，却不指任何具体的地理地点，只标着「哥伦比亚角」。指路柱是大副古休的手笔；柱臂底下装着一个带玻璃罩的小匣子似的装置，里面封存着皮尔里成功抵达北极点的行程记录，还有全队名册——我的名字也名列其中。从横臂的横杆上牵出一条条镀锌铁丝，牢牢系在沉重的岩石上，好帮助这座标碑顶住风暴的狂怒。博鲁普干得漂亮，还把成果拍了照；那石堆的照片一拿出来，指挥官看了十分满意。

5月27日，古德塞尔医生带着两支雪橇队——因纽特人克顺瓦和塔钦瓦（Tawchingwah）——离船前往黑曾湖（Lake Hazen）与拉格尔斯河（Ruggles River）一带行猎。他们在那一带顺利猎获了十三头麝牛；在风箱谷（Bellows Valley），他们又射杀了若干头「皮尔里」驯鹿，即学名*Rangifer Pearyi*的那个种——栖居该地区的一个独特的驯鹿类别。

===Original===
On the return of Dr. Goodsell, he told of his fascinating experiences in that wonderland. Leaving the *Roosevelt*, he had turned inland at Black Cliff Bay. Past the glaciers he went with his little party, down the Bellows Valley to the Ruggles River, an actual stream of clear-running water, alive with the finest of salmon trout. Adopting the Eskimo methods, he fished for these speckled beauties with joyful success. Here he rounded up and shot the herd of musk-oxen, and here he bagged his caribou. He was in a hunter’s paradise and made no haste to return, but crossed overland to Discovery Harbor and the barn-like structure of Fort Conger, the headquarters of General Greely’s “Lady Franklin Bay Expedition” of 1882﻿–﻿1883. Professor MacMillan was on his way to Fort Conger and it was with much surprise, on arriving there, that he found that Dr. Goodsell had reached it an hour before him. It was an unexpected meeting and quite a pleasure to the Professor to find the Doctor there, ready to offer him the hospitality of the fort.

Dr. Goodsell returned to the *Roosevelt* on June 15, with a load of geological, zoological, and botanical specimens almost as heavy as the loads of meat and skins he brought in. He was an ardent scientist, and viewed nearly every situation and object from the viewpoint of the scientist. Nothing escaped him; a peculiar form of rock or plant, the different features of the animal life, all received his close and eager attention, and he had the faculty of imparting his knowledge to others, like the born teacher that he was. He evinced an eager interest in the Eskimos and got along famously with them.

His physical equipment was the finest; a giant in stature and strength, but withal the gentlest of men having an even, mellow disposition that never was ruffled. In the field the previous spring he had accompanied the expedition beyond the “Big Lead” to 84° 29′, and with the strength of his broad shoulders he had pickaxed the way.

On account of his calm, quiet manner I had hesitated to form an opinion of him at first, but you can rest assured this was a “Tenderfoot” who made good.

During this time I left the ship on short hunting trips, but I was never away from the ship for more than ten or twelve hours.

===Chinese===
古德塞尔医生回来后，讲述了他在这片奇境中种种迷人的经历。离开*罗斯福号*，他从黑崖湾（Black Cliff Bay）折入内陆，带着自己那一小队人走过一道道冰川，顺风箱谷而下，来到拉格尔斯河边——那可是一条真正淌着清水的河流，满河都是上好的鲑鳟。他采用因纽特人的钓法钓这些斑驳的美物，竿竿得手，其乐陶陶。就在这里，他围住并射杀了那群麝牛；也是在这里，他猎获了自己的驯鹿。他置身猎人的天堂，全不急着回去，索性取道陆路横穿到发现港（Discovery Harbor），来到康格堡那座谷仓似的建筑——1882至1883年间格里利将军（General Greely）「富兰克林夫人湾探险队」的大本营。麦克米伦教授当时正走在去康格堡的路上，到达后却大为惊讶地发现，古德塞尔医生竟比他早到了一个钟头。这场不期而遇令教授喜出望外：医生已在堡中，正准备尽堡主之谊款待他。

6月15日，古德塞尔医生带着满满一载荷的地质、动物与植物标本回到*罗斯福号*上，那些标本几乎同他运回的一橇橇肉与皮张一样沉。他是个热忱的科学家，几乎无论什么情况、什么物件，都要用科学家的眼光去看。什么都逃不过他：一块形状奇特的岩石或植物，动物界的种种形态特征，无不受到他细致而热切的关注；而且他天生是块当教师的料，有本事把自己的知识传授给别人。他对因纽特人显示出浓厚的兴趣，同他们相处得极为融洽。

他的体格条件是第一流的：身量魁伟，膂力过人，却又是我见过最温和的人之一，性情平顺醇厚，从不着恼。前一年春天在冰原上，他曾随探险队越过「大水道」推进到北纬84度29分，凭着他宽阔双肩的力气，一路抡着冰镐开出路径。

起初，只因他为人沉静安详，我一直踌躇着不敢对他下断语；不过请放心，这位「新手」（tenderfoot）可是实实在在干出名堂来了。

这期间，我也离船作过几次短途行猎，但从没有一次离开船超过十个乃至十二个钟头。

===Original===
---

On July 1 quite a lead was opened in the channel south from Cape Sheridan to Cape Rawson. The ice was slowly moving southward, and the prospects for freeing the *Roosevelt* and getting her started on her homeward way were commencing to brighten. The following day a new lead opened much nearer shore, and on July 3 the Eskimos, who had been out hunting, returned from Black Cliff Bay, without game, but with the good news that as far south as Dumb Bell Bay there stretched a lead of open water. July 4, a new lead opened very close to the *Roosevelt*. The spring tides, with a strong southerly wind, had set in so very much earlier, three years before, that on July 4, 1906, the *Roosevelt* had been entirely free of ice, with clear, open water for quite a distance to the south; but this year the ship was still completely packed in the ice, and furthermore she was listed at the same angle as during the winter.

On July 5, I was detailed to help Gushue repair the more or less damaged whaleboats. The heavy and solidly packed snow of the winter had stove them in. On July 6, the anniversary of our departure from New York a year before, the greater part of the day was spent in pumping water from the top of a heavy floeberg into the ship’s boilers. This work was not completed until the morning of the 7th, when the fires were started. Due to the cold, the process of getting up steam was slow work. The ice had been breaking up daily, new leads were noticed, and on this day, July 7, a new lead opened at a distance of fifty yards from the ship, and open water stretched as far south as the eye could see. All hands were put to work reloading the supplies that had been placed on shore the fall previous, for it was easy to see that the time for departure was at hand.

With the boilers in order, an attempt was made to revolve the shaft, but the propeller was too securely frozen in the ice to move, and so Captain Bartlett got out the dynamite and succeeded in freeing the bronze blades.

===Chinese===
---

7月1日，谢里登角以南通往罗森角（Cape Rawson）的海峡里，裂开了一条相当宽的水道。冰在缓缓南移，让*罗斯福号*脱困启程、踏上归途的前景开始露出亮色。第二天，一条新水道在离岸近得多的地方裂开。7月3日，出外行猎的因纽特人从黑崖湾归来，猎物一无所获，却带回好消息：往南直到哑铃湾（Dumb Bell Bay），绵延着一条开阔的水道。7月4日，*罗斯福号*近旁也裂开了一条新水道。三年前，大潮连同强劲的南风来得比这早得多，以至1906年7月4日那天，*罗斯福号*已完全脱冰，往南相当远的一段都是清澈的开阔水面；可今年，船仍被冰结结实实地裹着，而且船身还保持着入冬以来那个角度的倾侧。

7月5日，我奉命去协助古休修理那些损坏程度不一的捕鲸艇。一冬又厚又结实的积雪，把艇身压得塌陷了。7月6日，是我们一年前从纽约启程的周年纪念日，这一天大半工夫都用来从一座厚重浮冰山的顶上抽水，灌进船上的锅炉。这项工作直到7日早晨才收尾，随即便升了火。天气严寒，烧足蒸汽是个缓慢的活计。冰连日崩解，新的水道不断出现；就在这一天——7月7日——离船五十码远处又裂开一条新水道，开阔的水面向南一直延伸到目力尽头。全体人员都被派去重新装载头年秋天存放到岸上的物资，因为谁都看得出来，启碇的日子已经到了眼前。

锅炉收拾停当后，船上试着转动轮轴，但螺旋桨在冰里冻得纹丝不动，于是巴特利特船长搬出炸药，终于把青铜桨叶炸脱了出来。

===Original===
From the 10th of July to the 13th, a fierce storm raged, clouds of freeing spray broke over the ship, encasing her in a coat of icy mail, and the tempest forced all of the ice out of the lower end of the channel and beyond as far as the eye could see, but the *Roosevelt* still remained surrounded by ice.

The morning of the 15th, a smart breeze from the northeast was blowing, and proved of valuable assistance to us, for it caused the huge blocks of ice that were surrounding the ship to loosen their hold, and for the first time since October, 1908, the *Roosevelt* righted herself to an even keel.

By this time all of our supplies had been loaded and stored, and from the crow’s-nest a stretch of open water could be seen as far as Cape Rawson. From there to Cape Union the ice was packed solid.

===Chinese===
从7月10日到13日，一场狂猛的风暴肆虐不休，一团团冻结的飞沫扑上船来，给船身裹上一层冰的铠甲；风暴把海峡下端的冰全部驱赶出去，一直赶到目力尽头之外，可*罗斯福号*依然被冰团团围住。

15日早晨，东北方吹来一阵劲风，帮了我们大忙：它让围在船四周的巨大冰块松开了钳制；自1908年10月以来，*罗斯福号*头一回回正了身子，龙骨恢复了水平。

这时，我们全部的物资都已装舱入库；从桅顶瞭望台望出去，开阔的水面一直可以望到罗森角。从罗森角到联合角（Cape Union）之间，冰仍旧冻结得严严实实。

## XX / 二十

===Original===
Two narrow escapes﻿—Arrival at Etah﻿—Harry Whitney﻿—Dr. Cook’s claims.
From Karnah the *Roosevelt* sailed to Itiblu, where hunting-parties secured thirty-one walrus and one seal. By the 11th of August we had reached the northern shore of Northumberland Island, where we were delayed by storm. It was shortly before noon of this day that we barely escaped another fatal calamity.
Chief Wardwell, while cleaning the rifle of Commander Peary, had the misfortune to have the piece explode while in his hands. From some unknown cause a cartridge was discharged, the projectile pierced two thick partitions of inch-and-a-half pine, and penetrated the cabin occupied by Professor MacMillan and Mr. Borup. The billet of that bullet was the shoulder and forearm of Professor MacMillan, who at the time was sound asleep in his berth. He had been lying with his arm doubled and his head resting on his hand. A half inch nearer and the bullet would have entered his brain.
As is always the case with narrow escapes, I, too, had a narrow escape, for that same bullet entered the partition on its death-dealing mission at identically the same spot where a few minutes previously *my* head had rested. Dr. Goodsell was quickly aroused, he attended Professor MacMillan, and in a short time he diagnosed the case as a “gunshot wound.” Finding no bones broken, or veins or arteries open, he soon had the Professor bandaged and comfortable.

===Chinese===
两次死里逃生——抵达埃塔——哈里·惠特尼——库克医生的说法。
从卡尔纳出发，*罗斯福号*驶往伊蒂布卢（Itiblu），猎队在那一带捕得三十一头海象和一只海豹。到8月11日，我们已抵达诺森伯兰岛（Northumberland Island）北岸，在那里被风暴耽搁下来。就在这天中午前不久，我们险些又撞上一场致命的大祸。
轮机长沃德维尔在擦拭皮尔里指挥官的步枪时，不幸枪在他手里走了火。不知什么缘故，一发子弹被击发，弹头穿透两道一英寸半厚的松木隔板，钻进了麦克米伦教授和博鲁普先生居住的舱室。那颗子弹的归宿，正是当时正在铺位上酣睡的麦克米伦教授的肩膀和小臂。他躺着的姿势是胳膊弯着、头枕在手上。再偏近半英寸，子弹就进他的脑袋了。
险死还生的事向来如此，我也同样逃过一劫：就在几分钟前，*我的*头枕的正是那同一个位置，而那颗子弹怀着杀人的使命，恰好从同一点上穿进了隔板。古德塞尔医生很快被唤醒，他为麦克米伦教授诊治，不多时便将此症诊断为“枪伤”。查明骨头未断、静脉动脉也完好无损，他很快替教授包扎停当，让他安适地歇下了。

===Original===
At the time of the accident to Professor MacMillan the ship was riding at anchor, but with insufficient slack-way, so in the afternoon, when the excitement had somewhat abated, Captain Bob decided to give the ship more chain, for a storm was imminent, and he gave the order accordingly. The boatswain, in his haste to execute the order, and overestimating the amount of chain in the locker, permitted all of it to run overboard. We were in a predicament, with the storm upon us, no anchor to hold the boat, and a savage, rocky shore on which we were in danger of being wrecked. There was a small five-hundred-pound anchor with a nine-inch cable of about one hundred and fifty fathoms remaining, which was repeatedly tried, but the ship was too much for this featherweight anchor, and dragged it at will. Commander Peary, with his usual foresight, had ordered steam as soon as the approach of the storm was noticed, and now that the steam was up, he ordered that the ship be kept head-on, and steam up and down the coast until the storm abated. The storm lasted until the night of August 13, and the best part of the following day was spent by two boat-crews of twelve men, in grappling for the lost anchor and chain, and not until they had secured it and restored it once more to its locker were they permitted to rest. With the anchor secure, walrus-hunting commenced afresh, and on the ice-floes between Hakluyt and Northumberland Islands thirty more walrus were secured.
On August 16, the *Roosevelt* steamed back to Karnah, and the Eskimo people who intended living there for the following winter were landed. A very large supply of meat was landed also; in addition to the meat quite a number of useful presents, hatchets, knives, needles, some boards for the making and repairing of sledges, and some wood for lance- and harpoon-staves, and a box full of soap were landed. This inventory of presents may seem cheap and paltry to you, but to these natives such presents as we made were more appreciated than the gift of many dollars would be by a poverty-stricken family in this country. With the materials that Commander Peary furnished would be made the weapons of the chase, the tools of the seamstress, and the implements of the homemaker. The Eskimos have always known how to utilize every factor furnished by nature, and what has been given to them by the Commander has been given with the simple idea of helping them to make their life easier, and proves again the axiom, “The Lord helps those who help themselves.”
After disembarking the Karnah contingent, the ship steamed to Etah, arriving there on the afternoon of August 17. As the *Roosevelt* was entering the harbor of Etah, all hands were on deck and on the lookout, for it was here that we were again to come in touch with the world we had left behind a year before. A large number of Eskimos were running up and down the shore, but there was no sign of the expected ship. Quickly a boat was lowered, and I saw to it that I was a member of the crew of that boat, and when we reached the beach the first person to greet me was old Panikpah, greasy, smiling, and happy as if I were his own son. I quickly recognized my old friend Pooadloonah, who greeted me with a merry laugh, and my misgivings as to the fate of this precious pair were dispelled. If you will remember, Panikpah and Pooadloonah were the two Eskimos who found, when on our Poleward journey, just about the time we had struck the “Big Lead,” that there were a couple of fox-traps, or something like that, that they had forgotten to attend to, and that it was extremely necessary for them to go back and square up their accounts. Here they were, fat, smiling, and healthy; and I apprehend somewhat surprised to see us, but they bluffed it out well.

===Chinese===
麦克米伦教授出事时，船正抛锚停泊，只是锚链放得不够宽余。于是当天下午，等众人的激动稍稍平息，巴特利特船长决定给船多放出些锚链——风暴已迫在眉睫——他随即下令。水手长急急执行命令，又把锚链舱里所存的链量估得过高，竟任凭链子全部滑出了船外。我们陷入了窘境：风暴压顶，却没有锚稳住船，而且沿岸是一片狰狞的礁石海岸，我们随时有在那上头撞碎船身的危险。船上还剩一只五百磅的小锚，配着一条九英寸粗、约一百五十英寻长的锚链，屡屡抛下试用，无奈这条船的分量远非这只轻量级小锚镇得住，反倒拖着它随心所欲地漂走。皮尔里指挥官素来预见在先，风暴将临的迹象刚一露头，他便吩咐烧汽；此刻汽压已足，他下令让船头迎着风浪，沿岸来回开行，直到风暴平息。这场风暴一直刮到8月13日夜里；第二天的大半天，两艇十二名船员都在用抓钩打捞失落的锚和锚链，直到把失物捞获、重新安放回锚链舱，才准他们歇息。锚既复得，猎海象的活计重新开场，在哈克卢特岛（Hakluyt Island）和诺森伯兰岛之间的浮冰上，又猎得了三十头海象。
8月16日，*罗斯福号*开回卡尔纳，把打算来年在那里过冬的因纽特人送上了岸。一大批肉食也随之卸岸；除肉以外，还有不少实惠的礼物：手斧、刀子、缝针，若干块制作和修理雪橇用的木板，一些做鱼镖杆和鱼叉杆的木料，还有满满一箱肥皂。这份礼物清单在你看来也许寒酸得可怜，但对这些土著来说，我们送上的这些礼物，比把许多美元送给本国一户穷苦人家还要让他们珍视感激。用皮尔里指挥官提供的这些材料，可以制成行猎的武器、缝衣妇的工具、当家人的用具。因纽特人向来深谙利用自然所赐的每一样东西；指挥官送给他们的东西，用心也简单，不过帮他们把日子过得松快些——这再度印证了那句格言：“天助自助者。”
卸下卡尔纳这批人之后，船向埃塔开去，8月17日下午抵达。*罗斯福号*驶入埃塔海港时，全船人都在甲板上瞭望，因为正是在这里，我们要重新接触到一年前抛在身后的那个世界。一大群因纽特人在岸上跑来跑去，却不见预期中那艘船的影子。小艇很快放了下来，我设法让自己成了这条艇上的艇员之一。我们一到滩头，头一个迎上来招呼我的就是老帕尼克帕，油光光的，笑呵呵，高兴得就像我是他的亲儿子。我随即认出了老朋友普阿德卢纳，他一阵欢笑同我招呼，我心里对这对活宝安危的疑虑顿时烟消云散。你大概还记得，帕尼克帕和普阿德卢纳就是北进途中那两个因纽特人：差不多在我们撞上「大水道」的时候，他们发现还有几个狐狸陷阱还是什么的忘了照料，觉得极有必要回去把这些账目料理清楚。这不，他们就在这儿，肥肥胖胖、笑眯眯、结结实实；我料想他们见到我们也有些吃惊，不过他们硬装镇定，装得还挺到家。

===Original===
Murphy and the young man Pritchard were also here. Murphy and Pritchard were the members of the crew who had been left here to guard the provisions of the expedition, and to trade with the Eskimos. Another person also was there to greet us; but who had kept himself alive and well by his own pluck and clear grit, and who reported on meeting the Commander of having had a most satisfactory and enjoyable experience. I refer to Mr. Harry Whitney, the young man from New Haven, Conn., who had elected at the last hour, the previous autumn, to remain at Etah, to hunt the big game of the region. When the *Roosevelt* had sailed north from Etah, the previous August, he had been left absolutely alone; the *Erik* had sailed for home, and there was no way out of this desolate land for him until the relief ship came north the following year, or the *Roosevelt* came south to take him aboard. His outfit and equipment were sufficient for him and complete, but he had shared it with the natives until it was exhausted, and after that he had reverted to the life of the aborigines. When the *Roosevelt* reached Etah, Mr. Whitney was an Eskimo; but within one hour, he had a bath, a shave, and a haircut, and was the same mild-mannered gentleman that we had left there in the fall. He had gratified his ambitions in shooting musk-oxen, but he had not killed a single polar bear.
At Etah there were two boys, Etookahshoo and Ahpellah, boys about sixteen or seventeen years old, who had been with Dr. Cook for a year, or ever since he had crossed the channel to Ellesmere Land and returned again. These boys are the two he claims accompanied him to the North Pole. To us, up there at Etah, such a story was so ridiculous and absurd that we simply laughed at it. We knew Dr. Cook and his abilities; he had been the surgeon on two of Peary’s expeditions and, aside from his medical ability, we had no faith in him whatever. He was not even good for a day’s work, and the idea of his making such an astounding claim as having reached the Pole was so ludicrous that, after our laugh, we dropped the matter altogether.
On account of the worldwide controversy his story has caused, I will quote from my diary the impressions noted in regard to him:

===Chinese===
墨菲和年轻人普里查德也在这里。墨菲和普里查德就是被留下来看守探险队给养、同因纽特人做交易的船员。还有另一个人也前来迎接我们——这位全凭自己的胆气和不掺假的硬骨气，保全了性命，活得健健康康，见到指挥官便报告说，自己度过了一段再称心快活不过的时光。我说的正是哈里·惠特尼先生，那位来自康涅狄格州纽黑文（New Haven, Conn.）的年轻人：头年秋天，他在最后一刻决定留在埃塔，猎取这一带的大猎物。前一年8月*罗斯福号*从埃塔北上一走，他就被孤零零一个人撂在了这里；*埃里克号*已启程回国，对他来说，除非来年补给船北上、或者*罗斯福号*南下接他登船，否则没有一点指望离开这片荒凉的土地。他的行装物资本来够他一人受用而且齐备，可他拿去同土著们分享，直到用尽为止；打那以后，他就回归了土著人的生活。*罗斯福号*抵达埃塔时，惠特尼先生已然是个因纽特人；可是不出一个钟头，他洗了澡、刮了脸、理了发，又成了我们秋天离开时那位温文尔雅的绅士。他猎麝牛的抱负已经得偿，只是一头北极熊也不曾猎到。
埃塔这里还有两个小伙子，埃托卡舒（Etookahshoo）和阿佩拉（Ahpellah），都是十六七岁的年纪，跟着库克医生有一年了——自从他渡海去埃尔斯米尔地再折返回来，两人便一直在他身边。这两个小伙子，就是他声称伴随他到过北极点的那两个人。在我们这些身在埃塔的人听来，这样的故事荒唐透顶，我们干脆一笑置之。我们了解库克医生，也了解他的能耐：他曾在皮尔里的两次探险队里当随队医生，而除了医道之外，我们对他一概不信。他连一天的活计都顶不下来，竟提出到过北极点这样骇人听闻的说法——这个念头可笑到这般地步，我们笑过之后，索性把这事整个丢开了。
鉴于他的故事在全世界激起的那场争论，我从自己的日记里抄录下当时记下的、关于他的印象：

===Original===
“August 17, 1909, Etah, North Greenland.
“Mr. Harry Whitney came aboard with the boatswain and the cabin-boy, who had been left here last fall on our way to Cape Sheridan. Murphy is the boatswain and Pritchard the boy, both from Newfoundland, and they look none the worse for wear, in spite of the long time they have spent here. Mr. Whitney is the gentleman who came up on the *Erik* last year, and at the last moment decided to spend the winter with the natives. He had a long talk with the Commander before we left for the north, and has had quite a lengthy session with him since. I learn that Dr. Cook came over from Ellesmere Land with his two boys, Etookahshoo and Ahpellah, and in a confidential conversation with Mr. Whitney made the statement that he had reached the North Pole. Professor MacMillan and I have talked to his two boys and have learned that there is no foundation in fact for such a statement, and the Captain and others of the expedition have questioned them, and if they were out on the ice of the Arctic Ocean it was only for a very short distance, not more than twenty or twenty-five miles. The boys are positive in this statement, and my own boys, Ootah and Ooqueah, have talked to them also, and get the same replies. It is a fact that they had a very hard time and were reduced to low limits, but they have not been any distance north, and the Commander and the rest of us are in the humor to regard Mr. Whitney as a person who has been hoodwinked. We know Dr. Cook very well and also his reputation, and we know that he was never good for a hard day’s work; in fact he was not up to the average, and he is no hand at all in making the most of his resources. He probably has spun this yarn to Mr. Whitney and the boatswain to make himself look big to them.
“The Commander will not permit Mr. Whitney to bring any of the Dr. Cook effects aboard the *Roosevelt* and they have been left in a cache on shore. Koolootingwah is here again, after his trip to North Star Bay with Dr. Cook, and tells an amusing story of his experience.”

===Chinese===
“1909年8月17日，北格陵兰，埃塔。
“哈里·惠特尼先生同水手长和舱室侍者一道上了船——去年秋天我们北上谢里登角的途中，这两人被留在了这里。墨菲就是那位水手长，普里查德就是那个侍者，两人都是纽芬兰（Newfoundland）人；虽然在这里待了这么久，看上去却毫无亏损。惠特尼先生就是去年乘*埃里克号*上来、又在最后一刻决定同土著们一起过冬的那位绅士。我们北上之前，他同指挥官作过长谈；我们回来之后，又同指挥官密谈了颇有一阵。我得知，库克医生带着他的两个小伙子埃托卡舒和阿佩拉从埃尔斯米尔地过来了，并在与惠特尼先生的一次私下谈话中声称，他到过了北极点。麦克米伦教授和我同他的两个小伙子谈过话，得知这种说法毫无事实根据；船长和探险队的其他一些人也都盘问过他们：就算他们到过北冰洋的冰面，也只是短短一程，不过二十或二十五英里。两个小伙子在这话上说得斩钉截铁；我自己的两个小伙子乌塔和乌奎亚也同他们谈过，得到的答复一模一样。他们确实吃了大苦头，被逼到了山穷水尽的地步，但他们不曾向北走出多远；指挥官和我们其余这些人，眼下都倾向于把惠特尼先生看作一个受了蒙蔽的人。我们深知库克医生其人，也深知他的名声；我们知道他从来就顶不了一天重活；说实话，他连一般水准都够不上，而且压根儿不善于把手头的家当用到极致。他多半是向惠特尼先生和水手长编了这么一套瞎话，好让自己在他们眼里显得高大。
“指挥官不许惠特尼先生把库克医生的任何一件物品带上*罗斯福号*，那些东西都留在了岸上的一处储藏点里。库卢廷瓦随库克医生去过北星湾（North Star Bay）之后，如今又回到了这里，还讲起自己此番经历里一段逗人发笑的故事。”

===Original===
It is only from a sense of justice to Commander Peary and those who were with him that I have mentioned Dr. Cook. The outfitting of the hunting expedition of Mr. Bradley was well known to us. Captain Bartlett had directed it and had advised and arranged for the purchase of the Schooner *John R. Bradley* to carry the hunting party to the region where big game of the character Mr. Bradley wished to hunt could be found. We knew that Dr. Cook was accompanying Mr. Bradley, but we had no idea that the question of the discovery of the North Pole was to be involved.
I have reason to be grateful to Dr. Cook for favors received; I lived with his folks while I was suffering with my eyes, due to snow blindness, but I feel that all of the debts of gratitude have been liquidated by my silence in this controversy, and I will have nothing more to say in regard to him or to his claims.

===Chinese===
我之所以提到库克医生，完全是出于对皮尔里指挥官及随行诸公的一点公道之心。布拉德利先生那次狩猎探险的行装筹办，我们一清二楚：那是由巴特利特船长一手经办的，他还建议并安排购买了纵帆船*约翰·R·布拉德利号*（John R. Bradley），把狩猎队载往能猎得布拉德利先生想猎的那类大猎物的地区。我们知道库克医生随布拉德利先生同行，却万万没有想到，北极点的发现问题竟会牵涉在内。
我曾受惠于库克医生，有理由感激他：当年我因雪盲害了眼病，曾在他的家人家里住过一阵。但我觉得，在这场争论中我一直保持沉默，这笔笔人情债已经全部清偿；关于他其人、关于他的种种说法，我不想再多说一个字。

## XXI / 二十一

===Original===
Etah to New York﻿—Coming of mail and reporters﻿—Home!
At Etah we expected to meet the relief ship. Sixty tons of coal and a small quantity of provisions had been left there during the previous summer, to be used by us on our homeward voyage. This coal was loaded on board and the Eskimos who desired to remain at Etah were landed. Just at the time we were ready to sail a heavy storm of wind and snow blew up, and it was not until six p.m. on the 20th that we left the harbor. Farewells had been said to the Eskimos, all that had been promised them for faithful services had been given to them, and we commenced the final stage of our journey home.
From Etah, August 20, the ship sailed along the coast, landing Eskimos at the different settlements, and on the 23rd of August at two a.m., we met the Schooner *Jeanie*, of St. John, NF, commanded by Samuel Bartlett. The schooner was supplied with provisions and coal for the relief of the *Roosevelt*, and was executing the plan of the Peary Arctic Club.
There was mail aboard her and we had our first tidings of home and friends in a twelvemonth. From newspaper clippings I learned that the British Antarctic Expedition, commanded by Sir Ernest H. Shackleton, had reached within 111 miles of the South Pole.
The mail contained good news for all but one of us. Mr. Borup, in his bunk above the Professor’s, read his letters, and in the course of his reading was heard to emit a deep sigh, then to utter an agonizing groan. Prof. MacMillan, thinking that Borup had received bad news indeed, endeavored to console him, and at the same time asked what was the bad news, feeling sure it could be nothing less than the death of Colonel Borup or some other close relative of his.

===Chinese===
从埃塔到纽约——邮件与记者的到来——到家了！
在埃塔，我们等着与接应船会合。前一个夏天，有六十吨煤和少量口粮存放在那里，供我们归航途中取用。这批煤装上了船，愿意留在埃塔的因纽特人也送上了岸。偏偏在我们准备启航的时候，刮起了一场狂风大雪，直到20日下午六点，我们才驶出海港。同因纽特人已经一一作过别，为酬谢他们忠实效劳而许诺下的东西也都如数给了他们，我们由此踏上归程的最后一程。
8月20日，船从埃塔出发，沿海岸一路航行，把因纽特人送到各个定居点上岸。8月23日凌晨两点，我们遇见了来自纽芬兰圣约翰（St. John, NF）的双桅帆船珍妮号（Jeanie），船长是塞缪尔·巴特利特（Samuel Bartlett）。这艘帆船载着接济*罗斯福号*的口粮和煤，此行正是来执行皮尔里北极俱乐部的计划。
船上有邮件，我们得到了整整一年来关于家和亲友的第一批音讯。从几份剪报里我得知，由欧内斯特·H·沙克尔顿爵士（Sir Ernest H. Shackleton）率领的英国南极探险队，已经进抵离南极点111英里以内的地方。
邮件给我们人人都带来了好消息，唯独一个人例外。博鲁普先生的铺位在教授铺位的上方，他读信的时候，人们先听得他发出一声深长的叹息，继而又是一声痛楚的呻吟。麦克米伦教授以为博鲁普当真收到了噩耗，便一边宽慰他，一边问是什么坏消息，心里认定那少不得是博鲁普上校，或是他哪位至亲亡故了。

===Original===
“What is the matter, George? Tell me.”
“**Harvard beat Yale!**”
The *Roosevelt*, accompanied by her consort, sailed south to North Star Bay and while entering the harbor ran ashore. Late in the afternoon, however, the rising tide floated her. While waiting for the tide, a party of six, I among the number, went ashore and visited the Danish Missionary settlement established there, the Eskimos acting as our interpreters, we being unable to speak Danish and the missionaries being unable to speak English. It was in North Star Bay that the coal and provisions from the *Jeanie* were transferred to the *Roosevelt*.
Aboard the *Jeanie*, there was a young Eskimo man, Mene, who for the past twelve years had lived in New York City, but, overcome by a strong desire to live again in his own country, had been sent north by his friends in the States. He was almost destitute, having positively nothing in the way of an equipment to enable him to withstand the rigors of the country, and was no more fitted for the life he was to take up than any boy of eighteen or twenty would be, for he was but a little boy when he first left North Greenland. However, Commander Peary ordered that he be given a plentiful supply of furs to keep him warm, food, ammunition and loading outfit, traps and guns, but, I believe, he would have gladly returned with us, for it was a wistful farewell he made, and an Eskimo’s farewell is usually very barren of pathos.

===Chinese===
“怎么了，乔治？跟我说说吧。”
“**哈佛把耶鲁打败了！**”
*罗斯福号*偕僚船南下，驶往北星湾（North Star Bay），进港时搁了浅。不过到傍晚时分，上涨的潮水又把它浮了起来。趁等潮的工夫，一行六人——我也是其中之一——上了岸，参观了设在那里的丹麦传教士定居点；我们说不了丹麦语，传教士们说不了英语，便由因纽特人充任翻译。正是在北星湾，*珍妮号*上的煤和口粮转运到了*罗斯福号*上。
*珍妮号*上有个年轻的因纽特人，名叫梅内（Mene），过去十二年一直在纽约市生活，只因重返故土的念想太强烈，由他在美国的朋友们给送了回来。他几乎一贫如洗，全然没有足以抵御这片土地严寒的装备；论对将要开始的那种生活的准备，他也不比任何一个十八九岁的男孩更强，因为他当初离开北格陵兰时，还是个小孩子。尽管如此，皮尔里指挥官仍吩咐给他备下充足的御寒毛皮、食物、弹药和装弹用具、猎夹和枪支。不过我相信，他本是很乐意跟我们回去的，因为他道别时那份神情满是眷恋，而因纽特人的道别，通常是难得带什么伤感的。

===Original===
Mr. Whitney transferred his augmented equipment to the *Jeanie*, intending to remain with her down the Labrador, for her Captain had agreed to use every effort to help Mr. Whitney secure at least one polar bear.
Cape York was reached on the morning of August 25, and from the two Eskimo families, living at the extreme point of the Cape, we obtained the mail which had been left there by Captain Adams of the Dundee Whaling Fleet *Morning Star*. Our letters, although they bore no more recent a date than that of March 23, 1909, were eagerly read.
At Cape York we landed the last of the Eskimos. The decks were now cleared. The boats were securely lashed in their davits, and nine a.m., August 26, in a gale of wind, the *Roosevelt* put out to sea, homeward-bound, but not yet out of danger, for the gale increased so considerably that the *Roosevelt* was forced to lay to under reefed foresail, in the lee of the middle pack, until the 29th, when the storm subsided and the ship got under way again.
On September 4 the Labrador was sighted. Under full steam we passed the Farmyard, a group of small islands which lie off the coast.

===Chinese===
惠特尼先生把他扩充了的行装转移到*珍妮号*上，打算随船沿拉布拉多（Labrador）海岸南下，因为船长已经答应竭尽全力，帮他至少弄到一头北极熊。
8月25日清晨，我们抵达约克角，从住在海角尽头的两户因纽特人手里，取回了邓迪捕鲸船队晨星号（Morning Star）的亚当斯船长（Captain Adams）存放在那里的邮件。我们的信件，日期再新也新不过1909年3月23日，可大家仍读得迫不及待。
在约克角，最后一批因纽特人上了岸。甲板至此清空，小艇都牢牢捆缚在吊艇柱上。8月26日上午九点，*罗斯福号*顶着大风出海，踏上归途——不过危险还没有过去，因为风势愈来愈猛，*罗斯福号*被迫缩起前帆，在中段浮冰群的下风处滞航，直到29日风暴平息，船才重新开航。
9月4日，望见了拉布拉多。轮船开足马力，驶过农庄群岛（the Farmyard）——那是卧在海岸外的一群小岛。

===Original===
We arrived at Turnavik at seven-thirty p.m. Once again we saw signs of civilization. The men and women appeared in costumes of the Twentieth Century instead of the fur garments of the Eskimos. Here we loaded nineteen tons of coal. Here we feasted on fresh codfish, fresh vegetables, and other appetizing foods to which our palates had long been strangers.
You know the rest, for from Turnavik to Indian Harbor was only a few hours’ sailing.
At Indian Harbor was located the wireless telegraph station from where Commander Peary flashed to the civilized world his laconic message, “Stars and Stripes nailed to the North Pole.”
Within half an hour of our arrival, the British cutter *Fiona* entered the harbor and the officers came aboard the *Roosevelt*. Thereafter for every hour there was continuous excitement and reception of visitors.

===Chinese===
傍晚七点半，我们抵达图尔纳维克。又一次，我们见到了文明的迹象。男男女女穿着的是二十世纪的服装，而不是因纽特人的毛皮衣裳。在这里，我们装上了十九吨煤。在这里，我们饱餐了一顿新鲜的鳕鱼、新鲜的蔬菜，还有种种开胃的吃食——这些滋味，我们的舌头早已久违了。
余下的事你们都知道了，因为从图尔纳维克到印第安港（Indian Harbor），只有几个小时的航程。
印第安港设着一座无线电报局，皮尔里指挥官就是从那里向文明世界拍出了他那句言简意赅的电文：“星条旗已钉上北极点。”
我们抵达后不到半小时，英国缉私艇菲奥娜号（Fiona）便驶进港来，艇上军官登上*罗斯福号*拜访。此后每一个钟头都有接连不断的兴奋热闹，迎来一批批访客。

===Original===
On September 13th the steamer *Douglas H. Thomas*, of Sydney, C.B., arrived, having on board two representatives of the Associated Press, accompanied by Mr. Rood, a representative of *Harper’s Magazine*.
The next day the cable-boat *Tyrian* arrived, with seventeen newspaper reporters, five photographers, and one stenographer. The *Tyrian* anchored outside the harbor and in five lifeboats the party was brought aboard the *Roosevelt*. As they rowed they cheered, and when they sighted Commander Peary three ringing cheers and a tiger were given. The newspaper men requested an interview with the Commander. He granted their request, at the same time suggesting that they accompany him ashore to a fish-loft at the end of the pier, where there would be more room than aboard the ship. Accompanied by the members of the expedition, the Commander and the reporters left the ship. Arriving at the loft Commander Peary sat on some fishnets at the rear end of the loft, some of the reporters sat on barrels and nets, others squatted on the floor. They formed a semicircle around him and eagerly listened to the first telling of his stirring story.
Before leaving Battle Harbor, we received a visit from the great missionary, Dr. Grenfell, the effect of whose presence was almost like a benediction.
On the morning of the 18th we left Battle Harbor accompanied by the tug *Douglas H. Thomas*, amidst the salutes of the many vessels and boats in the harbor and the cannon on the hill.

===Chinese===
9月13日，来自悉尼（新斯科舍省）的轮船道格拉斯·H·托马斯号（Douglas H. Thomas）到了，船上载着美联社的两名代表，同来的还有《哈泼斯杂志》（Harper’s Magazine）的代表鲁德先生（Mr. Rood）。
第二天，布缆船提里安号（Tyrian）到了，载来十七名报社记者、五名摄影师和一名速记员。*提里安号*在港外抛锚，一行人分乘五条救生艇，被送上*罗斯福号*。他们一边划桨一边欢呼，望见皮尔里指挥官时，齐齐给了三声嘹亮的欢呼，外加一声“虎吼”。报界人士请求采访指挥官。他应允了，同时提议大家随他上岸，到栈桥尽头的一座晒鱼棚里去，那里比船上宽敞些。指挥官和记者们在探险队员的陪同下离了船。到了鱼棚，皮尔里指挥官在棚子后头的几张渔网上坐下，记者们有的坐在木桶和渔网上，有的蹲在地板上。众人围着他坐成半圈，热切地听他头一回讲述那段动人心魄的经历。
离开巴特尔港（Battle Harbor）之前，那位伟大的传教士格伦费尔医生（Dr. Grenfell）前来探访；他亲临其境的那份感染，几乎如同一场祝福。
18日早晨，我们在拖船*道格拉斯·H·托马斯号*的伴随下离开巴特尔港，港内众多大小船艇纷纷鸣笛致意，山上的礼炮也轰然作响。

===Original===
Through the Straits of Belle Isle we steamed, with a fair wind and a choppy sea. In the meantime I was busily engaged in making a strip to sew upon a large American flag. This was a broad white bar which was to extend from the upper right to the lower left corner of the flag, with the words “North Pole” sewed on it.
About six a.m. on the 21st, a large white, steam-yacht was seen approaching, flying an American flag from her foremast and the English flag from the mizzenmast. We were close enough to her to distinguish Mrs. Peary and the children on board. A boat was quickly lowered from the yacht and the Peary family was soon united aboard the *Roosevelt*.
All kinds of sailing craft now met the *Roosevelt* and by them she was escorted into the harbor of Sydney, C.B. Whistles were blown, thousands of people lined the shores of the harbor, cheering enthusiastically and waving flags, and as the *Roosevelt* was moored alongside the pier, a delegation of schoolgirls met the Commander, made an address, and presented him with a magnificent bouquet. The streets were gorgeously decorated and a holiday had been declared. A ripe, royal welcome was accorded the *Roosevelt* and the members of the expedition. Visitors boarded the ship and looted successfully for souvenirs.
It was at Sydney that the expedition commenced to disband. Commander Peary and his family returned to the United States via railroad-train.

===Chinese===
我们驶过贝尔岛海峡，一路顺风，海面浪涛细碎起伏。这当口我正忙着裁制一条布带，要缝到一面大幅美国国旗上去。那是一条宽宽的白杠，将从旗子的右上角斜贯到左下角，上面缝着“北极点”字样。
21日清晨六点来钟，望见一艘白色大型蒸汽游艇驶近而来，前桅上飘着美国旗，后桅上飘着英国旗。双方离得够近，认得出艇上皮尔里夫人和孩子们。游艇上迅速放下一只小艇，皮尔里一家转眼就在*罗斯福号*上团聚了。
这时，各式各样的船只纷纷迎上*罗斯福号*，簇拥着它驶进悉尼港。汽笛声声，成千上万的人挤满港湾两岸，热烈欢呼，挥舞旗帜。*罗斯福号*在码头边系泊停当，一群女学生代表迎上指挥官，致了辞，又献给他一大束华丽的鲜花。街道装点得五彩夺目，当天还宣布放假。*罗斯福号*和探险队员们受到了十足隆重、帝王般的欢迎。来访者们登船搜寻纪念品，个个满载而归。
探险队就是在悉尼开始解散的。皮尔里指挥官携家人乘火车返回美国。

===Original===
The *Roosevelt* left Sydney on September 22 for New York City. A stop was made at Eagle Island, in Casco Bay, off the coast of Maine, where is located the summer home of Commander Peary, and here we landed most of his paraphernalia, some sledges and dogs. From Eagle Island we steamed direct to Sandy Hook, reaching there at noon on October 2. The next day the *Roosevelt* took her place with the replica of those two historic ships, the *Half Moon* and the *Clermont*, in the lead of the great naval parade.
And now my story is ended; it is a tale that is told. “Now is Othello’s occupation gone.”
I long to see them all again! the brave, cheery companions of the trail of the North. I long to see again the lithe figure of my Commander! and to hear again his clear, ringing voice urging and encouraging me onward, with his “Well done, my boy.” I want to be with the party when they reach the untrod shores of Crocker Land; I yearn to be with those who reach the South Pole, the lure of the Arctic is tugging at my heart, to me the trail is calling!

> “The Old Trail!
 

 The Trail that is always New!”

===Chinese===
9月22日，*罗斯福号*离开悉尼，驶往纽约市。中途在伊格尔岛（Eagle Island）停靠了一站——那座岛位于缅因州海岸外的卡斯科湾（Casco Bay），皮尔里指挥官的消夏居所就设在那里——我们在此卸下了他的大部分行装什物，还有几副雪橇和几条狗。从伊格尔岛出发，我们径直开往桑迪胡克（Sandy Hook），于10月2日中午抵达。第二天，*罗斯福号*便同那两艘历史名船——半月号（Half Moon）与克莱蒙特号（Clermont）——的复制品一道，走在盛大海上阅兵的最前列。
而今我的故事讲完了；这段往事已然讲毕。“奥赛罗的事业完了。”
我渴望再见到他们所有人！那些北方征途上勇敢而快活的伙伴。我渴望再见到我的指挥官那矫健的身影！渴望再听到他那清亮铿锵的嗓音，一声声“干得好，我的孩子”，催我、励我，催我奋进。队伍踏上克罗克地（Crocker Land）那无人涉足的海岸时，我想同他们在一起；有人抵达南极点时，我也渴盼身在其间。北极的诱惑正牵扯着我的心；于我，征途正在召唤！

> “古老的征途！
 

 永远常新的征途！”
